### United States v. Hilario — 2001-03-19

**Souter, concurring in part and in the judgment.** Souter joins the statutory and interbranch holdings and affirms the judgment, but requires a fuller officer-classification inquiry than a sufficient finding of combined superior controls. His Edmond concurrence, drawing on Morrison, requires attention to duties, jurisdiction, tenure and removal as well as hierarchy. Ordinary federal prosecution entails substantial duties throughout a district, and Gil's prolonged, potentially indefinite tenure defeats reliance on a temporary label alone. Those considerations do not overcome the Attorney General's direct control of duties and individual cases, presidential removal and the statutory vacancy endpoint. Together they establish inferior status here. The additional inquiry supplies his sufficient classification ground, not an extra join in the Court's classification rationale. His conclusion neither makes long service a constitutional cure nor changes the limits on judicial participation in prosecution.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/United_States_v_Hilario_merits_2001-03-19.md#separate-writings). These positions are noncontrolling except for expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Shafer v. South Carolina — 2001-03-20

**Scalia, dissenting, joined by no other Justice.** He would affirm because due process does not authorize this additional constitutional parole-information requirement. His Simmons objection distinguishes Gardner's unavailable adverse sentencing material and Skipper's excluded rebuttal evidence from a general duty to explain parole law; Ramos preserves substantial state control of truthful sentencing information. He accepts the logical relation between Simmons and this actual death-or-life choice but would confine that precedent to its facts. Sound instructional policy does not itself establish a constitutional command. No affirmative State representation that ordinary release was available establishes the distinct misleading-assertion problem. He decides neither adequate compliance under the Court's rule nor harmlessness and does not join Thomas's dissent.

**Thomas, dissenting, joined by no other Justice.** Assuming Simmons applies, he finds its substance satisfied by the repeated judicial until-death definitions, the natural-life verdict description and counsel's permitted argument that Shafer would remain in prison and never go home. The questions about someone convicted of murder are compatible with inquiry about the judge's options if no aggravator is found; they do not necessarily contradict the jury's explained life alternative. In his view the supplemental response confined the jury to its own task without affirmatively suggesting ordinary release. Simmons permits substance to be conveyed without preferred words, and greater precision does not itself justify constitutional intervention. He would affirm for adequate opportunity even assuming future danger, not because danger was absent or error harmless. His disagreement with Simmons remains a reservation, not a second independent ground here. He makes no finding about jurors' actual beliefs and does not join Scalia's dissent.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Shafer_v_South_Carolina_merits_2001-03-20.md#separate-writings). Both are noncontrolling; neither Justice joins the other dissent.

### Circuit City Stores, Inc. v. Adams — 2001-03-21

**O'Connor, dissenting, joined by Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas.** These Justices would reverse the categorical employment exclusion and remand. They read the residual class by the transportation character shared by seamen and railroad employees, giving independent work to both the enumeration and section 1's narrower commerce formulation. The labor context cannot erase that enacted limitation; section 2's broader coverage language does not require an equally broad section 1 exclusion. Gilmer reserved the precise question and thus does not decide it, but neither does that reservation establish the broader exclusion.

Their disagreement concerns coverage, not automatic enforcement. They would preserve actual formation, assent, delegation, clause scope, genuinely general revocation defenses, federal jurisdiction and lawful injunction authority. No new cost hearing follows merely because a defense is conceivable, and Adams's state-law claims do not automatically acquire Green Tree's federal-statutory-access premise. The original federal compulsion suit is distinct from the state-court command challenged by Scalia and Thomas in Allied-Bruce; their join withdraws no such state-court position. Coverage would remove the Ninth Circuit's exclusion ground without automatically reinstating every part of the district court's order or deciding discrimination liability.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Circuit_City_Stores_v_Adams_merits_2001-03-21.md#separate-writings). The positions are noncontrolling except for expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Egelhoff v. Egelhoff — 2001-03-21

**Breyer, dissenting; Stevens joins the background-validity ground but not the separate recipient-only insurance ground.** These Justices would affirm rejection of preemption for both demands. The investment plan expressly contemplates invalid designations and supplies its own surviving-spouse-then-children fallback. The insurance plan supplies no contrary invalidity term. A defeasible state rule determining continued validity can therefore fill a documentary gap rather than contradict a settled plan instruction. Traditional family and property law does not automatically prevail, but Travelers and Dillingham require an actual federal conflict; uniform administration alone does not identify which background validity rules federal law excludes.

They distinguish Boggs's succession-based transfer of an existing interest in then-undistributed pension property from a rule determining whether the participant's own designation remains valid. The statute's opt-out, entitlement exceptions and payor protections matter to its operation. Neither the word wife nor the fallback proves automatic cancellation, incorporation of state law, or the children's entitlement. Their proposed affirmance would leave actual exceptions, validity and ordinary state-law application for the lower courts, without awarding benefits or adjudicating separate payor penalties.

**Breyer's additional insurance ground, not joined by Stevens:** The insurance has already been paid. A recovery confined to the recipient need not redirect future plan payments or impose a second payment on the plan, and welfare insurance does not carry pension anti-alienation protection. Breyer would also permit that confined recipient claim for the reasons of his Boggs separate position. He recognizes that the asserted right derives from divorce revocation rather than a newly discovered bargain; his disagreement is with treating that source as preemptive after payment. This alternative alone does not resolve the pension demand, whose distribution status is unknown.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Egelhoff_v_Egelhoff_merits_2001-03-21.md#separate-writings). The positions are noncontrolling except for expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Ferguson v. City of Charleston — 2001-03-21

**Scalia, dissenting, joined by Thomas.** These Justices would affirm on special needs even assuming an unconsented bodily search. The clinical screening and treatment referrals preceded police cooperation, and the district court found a genuine purpose of protecting mothers and infants. They consider that actually performed diagnostic and protective function independently sufficient when assessed against the testing intrusion. Adding reporting and prosecution to encourage compliance does not eliminate the medical function merely because it also serves enforcement.

Griffin demonstrates that a real protective or supervisory justification can coexist with police cooperation and subsequent prosecution. The dissent does not classify ordinary patients as probationers; it regards their distinct clinical circumstances as sufficient on this record. Its Edmond position similarly requires an actual independent function, not nominal legitimate checks attached to ordinary policing. The initial arrests without a treatment alternative remain a material fact, but the direct diagnostic and protective purpose was not exhausted by the later treatment-before-arrest option. Chandler's inadequate candidate-testing need and Vernonia's distinct program do not foreclose this independently justified medical operation.

This ground supplies no general permission for unsupported hospital evidence programs, no finding of identical indications for every test, and no final consent adjudication. Scalia does not accept the trial instruction as a universal requirement of three separately expressed permissions; the shared dissent nevertheless rests on special needs under the assumed insufficiency of consent. Thomas joins no separate abandonment or third-party-disclosure theory. Neither Justice supplies a new preservation finding or a patient-specific consent conclusion.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Ferguson_v_City_of_Charleston_merits_2001-03-21.md#separate-writings). The positions are noncontrolling except for expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Gibbs v. Babbitt — 2001-03-21

**Ginsburg, concurring in part and in the judgment, joined by Breyer.** These Justices join Part II and the complete remand. They continue to favor the broader, materially supported substantial-class-effects approach stated in their Morrison dissent. Actual interstate visitation, volunteer activity and organized scientific use depend on the living population, and preventing the class of takings reasonably protects that resource. A supported class-wide relationship does not require a fixed revenue figure or a substantial effect from each wolf. Future pelt speculation and national conservation importance alone are insufficient and unnecessary to their view. That broader ground has not become the Court's rule. Given the controlling connection framework, they affirmatively join examination of both complete Home Builders routes on the existing record, without finding either already established or authorizing an indefinite search for new evidence.

**Stevens, dissenting, joined by Souter.** These Justices would affirm under the same proposed broader class-effects rule. The actual interstate use and study of an irreplaceable living population support a rational, materially grounded substantial-effects judgment, and preventing killing fits protection of that resource. Their view retains substantiality, evidence, reasonable means and judicial review but rejects the Court's additional connection prerequisite. Their earlier view of economic habitat conversion does not establish that direct killing is economic, and their Solid Waste ecological position supplies no interstate red-wolf facts. They do not assert that several incomplete Home Builders theories add up to a complete one. Because their proposed rule supports affirmance, they do not join the existing-law remand.

**Scalia, concurring in part and dissenting in part, joined by Thomas.** These Justices join Part I and would reverse for declaratory or injunctive relief confined to the challenged private-land applications. They accept authority over local conduct genuinely necessary to protect a valid interstate regime, but the offered grounds establish no supported anti-evasion or material-impairment relationship. They retain their Home Builders objection to territorial incapacity as an independent source of commerce authority and do not join Part II's externality route. Morrison confirms that economic consequences cannot supply the missing connection and that failure of the offered theories does not entitle the government to search indefinitely for another. Their proposed relief neither invalidates the ESA generally nor authorizes killing contrary to other valid law, revisits Mann's prior conviction, or awards compensation.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Gibbs_v_Babbitt_merits_2001-03-21.md#separate-writings). The positions are noncontrolling except for expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Texas v. Cobb — 2001-04-02

**Scalia, dissenting, joined by O'Connor, Kennedy and Thomas.** These Justices would reverse and remand because the Sixth Amendment right had not attached to the separately defined murders. McNeil makes the right offense-specific. Blockburger and Brown provide a legal boundary that reaches the same offense and its greater or lesser included counterpart without depending on the prosecutor's first charging label. The burglary and the actual multiple-murder capital count each require an element the other lacks.

They accept that Moulton forbids circumventing counsel to obtain evidence for the charged prosecution. But that protection does not, in their view, attach a right before proceedings begin on every inseparable additional offense. The Court's episode inquiry replaces a legal boundary with uncertain factual judgments and extends the safeguard beyond the pending prosecution. Independent Miranda protection and continued exclusion of unlawfully elicited charged-offense evidence remain meaningful.

Because no right attached to the murder charge on their ground, they would leave the alternative Jackson waiver issue undecided. Their silence supplies neither endorsement of Jackson's rationale nor a new rule allowing any custodial waiver to defeat an asserted Sixth Amendment right. Their proposed reversal would remove the attachment ground and permit ordinary state proceedings on any remaining properly presented issues; it would not automatically reinstate a final death sentence or decide a new coercion claim.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Texas_v_Cobb_merits_2001-04-02.md#separate-writings). The positions are noncontrolling except for expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Wallis v. Spencer — 2001-04-02

**Stone-Zsela, concurring in part and concurring in the judgment.** The Chief Justice joins Parts I, II.A and III: the minimum emergency rule, municipal policy, fault and causation requirements, and preservation-first remand. He supports the City's federal-immunity disposition and the state-law remand without adding grounds. He also states the bodily-integrity rule he would apply if the independent examination claim is properly presented: absent genuine urgent medical need or imminent loss of evidence, an invasive investigative examination of these children requires valid parental consent or prior judicial authorization through an appropriate process. Lawful protective removal alone supplies neither authorization. Reasonable arrangements for parental participation may reflect a genuine examination need; a categorical exclusion is insufficient.

The child's own bodily rights matter independently of parental authority. The three-day interval and evidentiary purpose require an actual explanation of urgency. An additional bodily intrusion cannot be justified solely by the legality of the initial custody. This applies the ordinary distinction between judicial authorization and genuine exigency to the examination; it creates no universal strict-scrutiny framework for family administration. Institutional responsibility remains essential: a preserved City policy or omission must be connected to the examination, and moral responsibility for the overall episode cannot replace that proof.

He reserves whether the City-specific examination theory was preserved and supported, joins vacatur of its conclusive adjudication and does not find these examinations unlawful or award damages. His conditional standard is not a holding of the Court.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Wallis_v_Spencer_merits_2001-04-02.md#separate-writings). The positions are noncontrolling except for expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### United States v. Cleveland Indians Baseball Co. — 2001-04-17

**Stone-Zsela, concurring in the judgment; no other Justice joins.** He would derive the payment-year rule independently from the tax provisions' payment and annual-receipt structure. The regulations and settled administration confirm that reading; they do not supply the rule in place of enacted text. Nierotko's benefits account serves a different statutory function, so its treatment of similar words does not require identical tax attribution. Administrative consistency permits predictable payroll calculation but cannot cure contrary text. He preserves Auer and Thomas Jefferson University without relying on deference to decide an ambiguity in this case. He agrees that the employer refund based on earlier-year allocation fails and that any remaining work is computational implementation. His independent statutory rationale is not the Court's controlling explanation.

**Scalia, concurring in the judgment; no other Justice joins.** He views temporal allocation of this damages substitute as a statutory gap. Neither treating the payments as wages nor observing that money changed hands in 1994 makes ordinary wage-payment language itself decide that gap. Nierotko's corresponding paid-period language makes direct textual compulsion particularly doubtful. He rejects the Court's explanation that different tax and benefit inequities themselves justify different meanings, but finds payment-year taxation a reasonable administrative resolution through the actual regulations. He therefore agrees with the operative payment-year applications and reversal without joining the Court's complete explanation or Stone-Zsela's direct statutory construction. His approach preserves the agency's lawful bounds and leaves individual benefits outside this case.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/United_States_v_Cleveland_Indians_Baseball_merits_2001-04-17.md#separate-writings). The positions are noncontrolling except for expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### United States v. Weston — 2001-04-17

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela joins the Court and would require the advance defense inquiry whenever restoration is an actual purpose, even if an additional safety rationale is independently supported. Bodily justification cannot extinguish the distinct protection of consultation, preparation and trial participation. Riggins supplies the practical reason for addressing those effects before the intrusion; Harper's convicted-prisoner safety setting does not dispense with that inquiry. He requires the Government to support the proposed regimen, alternatives and safeguards as compatible with an effective defense, without perfect prediction, and requires later review of actual effects. The proposed rule does not decide prosecution-only ultimate authority, genuine emergencies or treatment solely for execution.

Justice Kennedy joins the Court's necessary minimum and preserves his Riggins position that ultimate restoration authorization requires an extraordinary showing against significant impairment of the accused's reactions, participation or assistance to counsel. Omission of the preliminary inquiry necessarily fails that position; performing it is not by itself enough. His stronger standard is not the Court's rule.

Justice Thomas, joined by Justice Scalia, joins Part I and concurs in the judgment. The established involuntary treatment and preserved direct liberty objection distinguish this prospective case from their objections to reversing a completed conviction on speculative trial prejudice in Riggins. The deficient present justification warrants relief without adopting Part II's advance Sixth Amendment requirement. A properly supported independent safety authorization may leave trial protection for its appropriate later proceeding; they do not authorize an incompetent or unfair trial.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/United_States_v_Weston_merits_2001-04-17.md#separate-writings). The positions are noncontrolling except for expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Easley v. Cromartie / Smallwood v. Cromartie — 2001-04-18

Justice Kennedy, joined by Justices O'Connor, Scalia and Thomas, concurs in immediate vacatur but dissents from the constitutional rule and remand limitation. Consistent with his separate position in Hunt, he would reconsider the rule that rejects predominant racial assignment as a cognizable injury and permit a claim when race predominates over ordinary districting criteria. The objection is governmental racial assignment, not unusual shape, racial awareness or a percentage alone. The common electorate does not, in this view, answer the unequal-treatment concern.

The four would return the case for adjudication under that proposed rule instead of foreclosing the predominance claim. They do not conclusively affirm every trial finding, decide the full justification question or certify compliance with Hunt's different mandate. O'Connor's join does not adopt a finding of predominance as her own final factual conclusion. The writing does not establish an independently preserved claim or its waiver and supplies no controlling replacement for the Court's five-Justice rule.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Easley_v_Cromartie_Smallwood_v_Cromartie_merits_2001-04-18.md#separate-writings). The positions are noncontrolling except for expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Shaw v. Murphy — 2001-04-18

All participating Justices join the Court's complete opinion. The rule-construction and preserved as-applied questions are stated in that opinion.

### Children's Healthcare Is a Legal Duty, Inc. v. Min De Parle — 2001-04-23

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela joins Part II, agrees with operational vacatur without an additional ground, and dissents from Part I. He would require equal consideration of otherwise comparable serious secular moral or ethical objections absent a difference relevant to the accommodation's purpose or administration. Sincerity, actual conflict, condition-based eligibility, equivalent covered services and public accountability would remain required; mere preference against treatment would not suffice. Agostini and Mitchell permit secular care, but in his view that answers what may be purchased, not who may receive it. Kiryas Joel's neutrality concern supports distinguishing a genuinely different religious burden from an unexplained religious preference. He would declare the criterion invalid at that scope and remand for the lawful choice between equal eligibility within the enacted program and appropriations or withdrawal of the discriminatory provision, with a transition fitted to established patient reliance and care needs. He would create no appropriation, order no repayment of lawful benefits and enjoin no separable secular care merely because a provider is religious.

Justice Souter, joined by Justices Stevens and Ginsburg, joins Parts II and III but would also invalidate the religious financial criterion, subject to lawful severability. Amos permits accommodations beyond free-exercise compulsion, but in their view it does not justify this exclusive affirmative-payment route as relief from a governmental restriction. Kiryas Joel distinguishes accommodation from anomalous religious privilege. Their facial objection requires no proof that a particular payment purchased prayer. They independently support the operational remand under Mitchell and Marshfield and do not presume devotional financing from nurses' faith or religious practice. They would not automatically extend benefits to secular objectors or terminate lawful services without statutory remedial analysis.

Justice Scalia, joined by Justices Kennedy and Thomas, joins Parts I and II but would affirm operational summary judgment on this record. Agostini and Mitchell require an identified publicly financed religious function; private prayer and possible diversion do not supply it. The Cook account does not link a particular public payment to devotion, and the earlier manual retains final intermediary responsibility. Consistent with their Marshfield position, formal proximity or religious setting does not substitute for actual governmental attribution. Their conclusion does not immunize a future proved payment for devotion or surrender of public review, and it does not assume that the examination exemption removes every objective source of information.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Childrens_Healthcare_Is_a_Legal_Duty_v_Min_De_Parle_merits_2001-04-23.md#separate-writings). The positions are noncontrolling except for expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Alexander v. Sandoval — 2001-04-24

Justice Thomas dissents, joined throughout by O'Connor, Scalia and Kennedy. They would reverse insofar as the injunction rests on this direct regulatory-effects action and remand for ordinary proceedings on genuinely preserved claims and defenses, without creating a claim or guaranteeing amendment.

The dissent applies Powell's directly controlling prospective-relief rule. Section 601 supplies the accepted private protection against intentional discrimination; section 602 directs agency implementation and public compliance machinery. The distinction between those statutory functions matters because a valid additional effects obligation does not itself demonstrate congressional authorization of the broader private action. Cannon permits contextual implication but does not establish every regulatory action, and Guardians and Choate do not conclusively settle this enforcement route. The beneficiary-and-implementation argument is substantial, but Powell already rejected it in this precise prospective setting. A request for an injunction cannot cure the absence of an authorized action; Franklin concerns relief after that antecedent question is answered.

The dissent preserves McClellan's contextual inquiry and Central Bank's actual received securities action. It adopts neither a universal express-action-clause requirement nor a categorical denial of every regulation's private significance. Its sufficient ground is Title VI-specific. Regulatory validity, intentional-discrimination enforcement and authorized agency remedies remain intact.

Statutory stare decisis adds a separate objection to the Court's reconsideration. Congress can alter the rule, and a different policy, a proved rather than alleged effect, the decision's youth and continued disagreement do not change the authorization question. The dissent does not find that a recipient actually arranged its affairs in reliance on Powell. It considers the Court's account of the existing duty insufficient to overcome the distinction between a duty and a private cause of action. It would leave the accepted regulations in force while withdrawing this action as the injunction's legal foundation. It reaches no separate funding-notice ground, finds no discriminatory intent and supplies no damages, funding sanction or section 1983 claim.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Alexander_v_Sandoval_merits_2001-04-24.md#separate-writings). These positions are noncontrolling except for the expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Atwater v. City of Lago Vista — 2001-04-24

**Ginsburg, joined by Stevens, O'Connor and Breyer, in Part II.** These four conclude that the supported plaintiff-favorable account fails the governing constitutional standard. Atwater supplied identifying information, was known locally, acknowledged the offense and arranged for a friend to take the children. General child-safety and appearance purposes do not establish why handcuffing, transport, booking and cell confinement served a sufficient added function. Missing physical papers remain relevant but do not establish inability to identify her, and the earlier warning supplies no recidivism premise. Booking cannot prove its own antecedent necessity merely by generating paperwork.

Their conclusion applies Ricci's functional rule to this record; it does not require citation in every case or impossibility of every alternative. Their Ricci application reservation was record-specific, and they do not repeat it here. They prefer reversal on this constitutional ground but expressly support the common vacatur because it removes the categorical judgment and returns actual disputes and preserved defenses under their rule. They do not join a reservation that the same established facts might suffice. Actual liability, March 1997 immunity, City responsibility and lawful relief remain distinct, with no speculative factual reopening or obligatory hearing.

**Stone-Zsela, concurring in Part I and in the judgment.** He joins the actual-function extension and categorical-rule correction but does not decide this custody's ultimate reasonableness. Missing papers may justify verification; they do not conclusively justify each later restraint, particularly in light of the supplied number, address and known residence. Berating and a routine booking preference alone cannot justify confinement. He would require the lower courts to determine whether supported identification or other practical enforcement needs justified the additional restraint, without inventing a verification failure or demanding a perfect alternative. His remand separately preserves conduct-date immunity and municipal policy and causation. He does not join Part II's present constitutional-insufficiency conclusion, award damages or disturb the conviction.

**Thomas, dissenting, joined by Scalia, Kennedy and Souter.** Ordinary processing of an observed criminal charge, with the actual bounded execution here, supplies a sufficient enforcement relationship. The restraint involved handcuffing, transportation, booking and approximately an hour of confinement pending bond, not an unrelated investigation or a claimed permission for any duration or method. Their Ricci dissent treated ordinary same-offense processing as legally meaningful without proof that citation was impossible. That earlier case's documents and continuing-business problem are not transferred as facts here. This is a legal appraisal of criminal processing, not a finding of a special identity, appearance or safety need or compulsory station procedure. Supplied identification and local residence weaken those distinct factual rationales without eliminating ordinary criminal enforcement. The four would affirm on constitutional merits, leaving immunity and municipal predicates unadjudicated. Excessive force, unrelated additional detention and unjustified duration retain their own limits; no fixed permissible interval follows.

**Scalia, additionally dissenting, joined by Souter.** They would also sustain an ordinary criminal-arrest rule under which probable cause for an observed criminal offense and ordinary enforcement processing suffice, subject to independent execution, duration and enforcement-relationship limits. They distinguish Ricci's civil violation and give administrable criminal-arrest authority independent weight. The rule does not depend on predictions that an identified local driver will evade court, on subjective motive or on search consequences. They reject an added individualized showing merely because punishment is fine-only. Kennedy and Thomas do not join this broader categorical proposition; their dissent rests on the narrower actual processing and execution ground.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Atwater_v_City_of_Lago_Vista_merits_2001-04-24.md#separate-writings). These positions are noncontrolling except for the expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Sterling v. Borough of Minersville — 2001-04-24

**O'Connor, concurring in part and dissenting in part, joined by Kennedy.** They join the bounded present right and the exclusion of Hoban's factual appeal, but would grant Wilinsky personal immunity on this damages theory. Sheets's completed dissemination of entrusted marital material differs from an unexecuted orientation ultimatum. Denius properly recognizes present protection but cannot fill the earlier gap. Walls's employment-questionnaire ruling and the distinction between threats and completed injury reinforced objective uncertainty in April 1997. A demand may have no legitimate policing purpose yet remain an insufficiently clear constitutional application for personal damages. They require particularized notice, not an identical case in every setting. Their proposed reversal would protect the reviewed individual damages theory, without denying all compelled-disclosure protection, resolving other claims or deciding municipal responsibility.

**Scalia, dissenting in part, joined by Thomas.** They join the factual-appellate boundary but reject the additional merits extension. Sheets and Denius bind at their actual holdings; Whalen's reservation and intimate subject matter do not independently establish every further substantive-due-process entitlement. The moral wrong or absence of a sound purpose does not replace identification of a constitutional source. They accept the supported alleged ultimatum for legal review and do not rely on factual disbelief, harmlessness or a supposed state remedy curing a federal violation. Independently, they would grant immunity because the preconduct law did not sufficiently establish this orientation-and-unexecuted-ultimatum application. Their reversal reaches the reviewed individual theory, not every defendant or remaining claim.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Sterling_v_Borough_of_Minersville_merits_2001-04-24.md#separate-writings). These positions are noncontrolling except for the expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Daniels v. United States — 2001-04-25

**Stone-Zsela, concurring.** He joins the common available-review holding, counsel exception and affirmance, and additionally adopts an affirmative faultless-loss exception as part of his governing access rule. Section 2255 review should remain available when meaningful original review was unavailable or frustrated through no fault of the prisoner, subject to applicable procedure, proof of constitutional defect and adverse effect on present custody. This is his present legal position, not merely a suggestion that an exception might be recognized later.

The distinction is between meaningful first review and repetitive litigation. Custis's forum rule and reservation do not establish that every completed conviction had an effective first opportunity, while Maleng's custody requirement must still be respected. A later enhancement creates a new incentive but does not make an earlier available remedy unavailable. Conversely, administrative inconvenience should not insulate a demonstrably unlawful current sentence from every authorized challenge. He does not equate every attorney failure with the prisoner's deliberate choice. Daniels identifies no complete counsel deprivation or external obstruction to meaningful first review, so even this affirmative exception does not allow his claims. Stone's exception has no controlling majority and is not borrowed from a decision in Coss. He orders no fresh sentencing or predicate invalidation.

**Scalia, concurring.** He joins the actual available-review rule and finality reasoning, but adds a statutory objection: section 2255's remedy for an unlawful federal sentence does not itself authorize adjudicating an outstanding state predicate's non-Gideon constitutional defect. Custis supports distinguishing an operative conviction from a collateral objection, although its actual sentencing holding did not already decide every expired-custody setting. A possible constitutional duty to provide review would concern the rendering jurisdiction and would not identify section 2255 as the remedy. He does not affirmatively endorse a section 2255 faultless-review exception. The Court's statement of what it has not decided is a scope description, not his agreement that such an exception exists. He orders no alternative forum remedy and decides no absent constitutional claim.

**Souter, dissenting, joined by Stevens and Ginsburg.** Section 2255 expressly authorizes review of the current federal sentence when constitutional error allegedly makes that sentence unlawful. Custis's restriction at the sentencing proceeding supplies no sufficient statutory basis for a new categorical exclusion from this express collateral remedy. Custis's available original-custody setting and reservation make the difference particularly important. The dissent's reading permits examination of an allegedly unlawful enhancement without treating Daniels as still in custody under the old state judgments.

Finality and stale evidence matter through applicable procedure and lawful proof rules; they do not justify the additional categorical bar. Parke preserves regularity and proof, Garlotte separates custody from remaining conditions, and Fiore and Glover distinguish an actual constitutional wrong and its causal effect from access requirements. The dissent preserves applicable defaults and does not automatically import all section 2254 exhaustion requirements into section 2255. It would reverse and remand for authorized consideration, not find either plea invalid, guarantee a hearing or order a lower sentence. The remaining qualifying predicates and the actual effect on lawful federal punishment would still require adjudication.

**Breyer, dissenting.** He also would reverse the categorical denial and remand, but on a distinct statutory and practical ground. Custis should be reconsidered: the sentencing scheme is better read to permit properly raised constitutional challenges to predicates at sentencing under lawful proof rules. His earlier opinion in United States v. Paleo supplies that approach. A prohibition at the original federal sentencing then generates restrictive or increasingly complex collateral exceptions; section 2255 is an awkward substitute for deciding the predicate issue when the enhancement is imposed. His disposition agreement with Souter does not make him an automatic join in every aspect of Souter's construction of section 2255. He proposes reconsideration, not an accomplished overruling, and establishes no plea defect, excuse of default, hearing entitlement or sentence reduction.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Daniels_v_United_States_merits_2001-04-25.md#separate-writings). These positions are noncontrolling except for the expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Randell v. Johnson — 2001-04-25

Justice Scalia concurs in Part I and in the judgment, joined by Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg. They agree that the complaint must be compared with the actual operative custody determination, that an independent execution injury is not automatically barred, and that this screening record cannot sustain categorical dismissal. Scalia's Edwards analysis requires the actual legal consequence, not a procedural label or assumption that every excess day contradicts the judicial sentence.

They retain the common-law-informed ground stated in their Heck concurrence: a damages claim necessarily contradicting an outstanding criminal or custody adjudication ordinarily requires invalidation even after release. Present habeas unavailability is not itself favorable termination. They regard the contradiction of an extant adjudication as distinct from general state-remedy exhaustion, and a remedial gap alone does not remove that concern.

The strongest objection is a claimant whose custody was too short or whose injury became knowable too late to obtain meaningful review. These four do not adopt the Court's affirmative exception on this undeveloped record; they would first resolve the actual determination and necessary-invalidity predicates and reserve a fully established genuinely impossible-review case. Their position does not establish deliberate delay, adequate state review, prior adjudication or actual unlawfulness here. They would remand for classification, preserve ordinary preclusion and defenses only when their predicates exist, and require no impossible universal showing about every possible state procedure. They join neither Parts II–III's new exception nor the conditional directions implementing it.

### C & L Enterprises, Inc. v. Citizen Band Potawatomi Indian Tribe of Oklahoma — 2001-04-30



Authority: [published separate writings](../records/C_L_Enterprises_v_Citizen_Band_Potawatomi_Indian_Tribe_merits_2001-04-30.md#separate-writings). These positions are noncontrolling except for the expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Cooper Industries, Inc. v. Leatherman Tool Group, Inc. — 2001-05-14

Scalia, joined by Thomas, concurs in the complete opinion and judgment on the premise that BMW supplies the binding substantive limit for this limited grant. Their agreement concerns the division between legal constitutional review and historical factfinding. They retain their opposition to BMW's substantive due-process foundation and willingness to reconsider it in a case properly presenting that issue. The Court's bounded reasoning does not require them to endorse that foundation or a universal rule of independent review for every mixed question.

Ginsburg, joined by Stone-Zsela, would affirm on the standard-of-review question. Appellate courts independently determine the governing law, correct impermissible premises and unsupported factual assumptions, and require actual review of amount. They may nevertheless review the trial judge's supported punitive assessment for abuse of discretion. The jury's appraisal of culpability, deterrence and the course of misconduct depends on the record heard by it and the trial judge. Gasperini's institutional respect for supported valuation informs this allocation despite its compensatory setting. Bajakajian's governmental forfeiture and Ornelas's search determination concern distinct tasks and do not command a second independent civil punitive valuation.

The dissent preserves BMW's substantive boundary despite disagreement with its foundation and Oberg's demand for meaningful amount review. An award is not immune merely because a judge sustained it. Disregarding guideposts, punishing lawful copying, inventing threatened losses or substituting wealth for a connection to the wrong remains correctable. But the limited grant establishes none of those supposed errors as facts and does not authorize selection of a preferred punitive figure. The ninety-to-one ratio alone does not decide whether this supported assessment must be replaced. The dissent would leave the reviewed judgment operative without deciding a new dollar ceiling or Oregon's share of the award.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Cooper_Industries_v_Leatherman_Tool_Group_merits_2001-05-14.md#separate-writings). These positions are noncontrolling except for the expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Major League Baseball Players Association v. Garvey — 2001-05-14

Ginsburg joins Parts I and II and the judgment. The sufficient ground is that the original award should not have been disturbed: the arbitrator was applying the submitted framework, and the court replaced his factual assessment. Restoring the denial of vacatur removes the directed award. She therefore withholds Part III's additional discussion of the remedy after a genuinely proper vacatur and does not independently endorse the arbitrator's factual accuracy.

Stevens dissents from summary disposition. The boundary between serious factual error and abandonment of the agreement, and the remedy after a valid finding of that abandonment, warrant plenary briefing and argument. First Options, Wright and Eastern Associated Coal preserve contractual limits without eliminating those questions. He would engage the lower court's record-based reasoning before deciding that it crossed the review boundary and would not declare the arbitrator free from serious error on this treatment alone. His position seeks fuller consideration, not an ultimate affirmance of the monetary award.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Major_League_Baseball_Players_Association_v_Garvey_summary_2001-05-14.md#separate-writings). These positions are noncontrolling except for the expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Rogers v. Tennessee — 2001-05-14

Scalia, joined by Stevens and Thomas, joins the Court's bounded fair-warning holding and adds that an acknowledged substantive offense restriction cannot be erased retrospectively merely because reform was foreseeable. Bouie protects the criminal law governing the act, not only an estimate of what judges may later wish to change. Carmell reinforces respect for existing legal conditions while remaining a legislative case. Ordinary application of existing law and prospective reform remain permissible. These three also agree that, even under the competing foreseeability framework, medical obsolescence, infrequent litigation and change elsewhere supply inadequate warning here. Their additional position does not make disputed historical theories of common-law judging necessary to the judgment.

Breyer's concurrence expressly adopts the unexpected-and-indefensible due-process framework stated by O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg and rejects automatic incorporation of every legislative category. He nonetheless finds the warning inadequate. A sound reason for future reform does not show that the existing Tennessee limitation had ceased to govern Rogers's earlier conduct. His concurrence rejects the State's application while preserving the legitimacy of ordinary common-law development; it does not join the stronger structural concurrence.

O'Connor, joined by Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg, would affirm. Their framework asks whether the common-law development was unexpected and indefensible by reference to preconduct law. They accept the limitation's survival but regard its limited local use, obsolete causal-proof rationale and supported preconduct legal landscape as making abandonment foreseeable. Prior existence and constitutional unexpectedness are distinct questions. Lanier's notice inquiry and the difference between legislative and judicial change permit this application in their view. They do not rely on the rejected statutory-repeal theory, the assault's immorality alone, subjective reliance, or later authorities to establish prior notice.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/Rogers_v_Tennessee_merits_2001-05-14.md#separate-writings). These positions are noncontrolling except for the expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### United States v. Oakland Cannabis Buyers' Cooperative — 2001-05-14



Authority: [published separate writings](../records/United_States_v_Oakland_Cannabis_Buyers_Cooperative_merits_2001-05-14.md#separate-writings). These positions are noncontrolling except for the expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### United States v. Valentine — 2001-05-14

O'Connor, joined by Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas, would affirm on the supported account showing no submission before the first grab. The response about whom the command concerned, charge toward the officer and attempted pushing do not objectively manifest yielding. With the recent in-person gun report, that concrete conduct supplied a particularized basis for restraint at the grab. Hodari D. distinguishes an unheeded command from seizure; their Wardlow positions permit grounded contextual appraisal of these specific actions rather than a categorical flight rule.

They acknowledge that genuine earlier submission would change the timing inquiry, but regard the reported sequence and absence of supporting evidence for the alleged pause as sufficient to resolve this case without inventing a factual dispute. They reserve initial-command sufficiency, the complete permitting theory and the legal effect of actual prior yielding; those reservations are not joins in the Court's holdings. They do not adopt the panel's minimum-duration premise, infer justification from the discovered gun or suggest that every charge or flight in a high-crime area suffices. Their difference with the Court concerns the proper disposition on this existing record. Scalia retains his objection to Ornelas's allocation while applying that governing rule here.

Authority: [published separate writings](../records/United_States_v_Valentine_merits_2001-05-14.md#separate-writings). These positions are noncontrolling except for the expressly identified joins in the Court opinion.

### Bartnicki v. Vopper / United States v. Vopper — 2001-05-21

**Breyer, concurring, joined by O'Connor.** They join the Court's opinion in full because it resolves this application without making truthful publication automatically superior to every privacy interest. The statutes protect private speech as well as privacy: people may speak less freely when an illegal interception can be disseminated without consequence. The competing public-speech interest is therefore assessed against a real constitutional and practical value, rather than against a mere dislike of publicity.

The recipients acted lawfully in obtaining the recording and did not cause its interception. The speakers were directing a public-school labor dispute, and the recorded discussion concerned apparent physical harm against board members. Those circumstances create an unusually strong public interest and reduce the legitimate claim to secrecy for this particular discussion. They do not establish a criminal true threat, a plan of violence, a continuing danger when broadcast, or surrender of every private conversation by people who take public roles.

Their Turner, Rice, Sheets, Denius and Sterling merits positions support actual review, lawful responsibility boundaries and serious protection of genuine confidentiality. They preserve legislative latitude to protect private conversation and to prevent the distinct harm of dissemination in materially different settings. They do not adopt automatic strict scrutiny whenever substantial public-speech and private-speech interests conflict, a closed balancing checklist, or a rule that deterrence can never justify liability absent complicity. Their additional explanation qualifies the reach of their agreement while remaining consistent with every proposition of the bounded Court opinion.

**Scalia, dissenting, joined by Thomas.** They would reverse the constitutional ruling and summary-judgment direction in both dockets, then return the claims for ordinary adjudication of statutory elements and preserved defenses. The law regulates the defendant's knowing disclosure as a separately harmful act; it does not merely attribute a stranger's interception to an innocent recipient. Dissemination enlarges the intrusion and undermines the privacy that permits candid conversation.

The source criterion does not select a viewpoint or message. Under the content-neutral framework, the dissent finds adequate justification in preventing the additional disclosure injury, reinforced by the reasonable legislative judgment that limiting downstream dissemination can reduce the reward for interception. They would not require lawmakers to accept a route around the prohibition through recipients who did not themselves conduct the tap. That argument is a judgment about permissible prevention, not a finding of a quantified deterrent effect or respondent participation.

They distinguish Daily Mail, Florida Star and Landmark because the information here remained private until unlawful interception and the knowing disclosure itself is prohibited. Cohen and Branzburg preserve application of valid general law to the press; Rice supplies no universal requirement that a discloser must have assisted the antecedent crime before any publication restriction can be justified. Their objections to constitutional extensions in Sheets, Denius and Sterling do not deny legislative authority to protect conversation. They accept the need for First Amendment review and the significance of lawful receipt and public relevance, but find those considerations insufficient to invalidate this application. Reversal would not establish intentional interception, the remaining statutory elements, damages or any criminal threat.

An existing authorized disposition and its scope, or an approved revised procedural premise and matching controlled position, is required before this matter can proceed.

**Lackawanna County District Attorney v. Coss:** The supported positions do not establish the required majority for one exact judgment and mandatory remand. Further lawful consideration establishing a supported common disposition, or authorization for an appropriate subsequent procedural step, is required.
